1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97).
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level. A mixed-source training pilot preserves Qwen OOD detection with preliminary Llama transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}).
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance reversal on human data.}
13	Classification supervision is associated with lower variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\times$ observed range, 3 seeds); on ToxicChat this reverses (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013). Frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Encoder capacity and lexical analysis.}
16	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD).
17	
18	\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}
19	Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp), attack-dependent label sensitivity ($d \approx 0$ on DD/FITD, $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack at $n$\,=\,10), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness primarily through optimization regularization, with residual semantic contribution on structurally complex attacks, paralleling self-supervised results where training structure matters more than label content~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. Unlike random-label memorization~\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, random-label \emph{pretraining} here produces transferable representations, suggesting that classification structure regularizes the space. Three extensions emerge: MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training despite single-LLM effectiveness, frozen probing near-parity coexists with 8\,pp end-to-end gaps, and the hierarchy reverses on human data.
20	
21	\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\ benign-side.}
22	OOD F1 figures throughout this paper reflect attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions: DAPT closes the attack-side gap (vanilla F1\,=\,0.28--0.31 $\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0 on OOD families). The orthogonal challenge is benign-side distribution shift. On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}); cross-LLM error decomposition confirms collapse is driven by false positives on unfamiliar benign text, not detection failure. A benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows FPR drops from 68.1\% to 0\% with as few as 10 conversations (95\% CI upper 6.1\%, $n$\,=\,49) while all OOD F1 remains $\geq$\,0.953: the two axes are orthogonal and independently addressable.
23	
24	\section{Conclusion}
25	
26	Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes. On the attack side, classification-based DAPT is the most robust adaptation for base-size encoders, providing robustness through optimization regularization rather than semantic learning; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. On the benign side, 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate distributional false positives (WildChat FPR 68.1\%\,$\rightarrow$\,0\%) without detection degradation. Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST-confirmed equivalence on DD/FITD at $n$\,=\,10; $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack); negative results on temporal order and label granularity further constrain which properties matter. Combined with 24.3$\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), the two-axis decomposition yields a practical deployment recipe that requires no target-domain attack data.
27	
28	\section{Limitations}
29	\label{sec:limitations}
30	
31	All multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}).
32	
33	OOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. Residual calibration error (ECE\,=\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.
34	
35	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.
36	
37	\section*{Ethics Statement}
38	
39	This work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.
40	
```

```text
1	% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	
5	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.
6	
7	Existing defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}
8	
9	Pretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\ DeepContext) drops from F1\,=\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.
10	
11	Our pipeline, \textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants systematically vary training objectives while sharing the same two-stage pipeline (Section~\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.
12	
13	\begin{figure}[t]
14	\centering
15	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}
16	\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}
17	\label{fig:architecture}
18	\end{figure}
19	
20	Controlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions reveals two orthogonal generalization axes. On the \emph{attack side}, vanilla DeBERTa drops to F1\,=\,0.28--0.31 on OOD attacks; classification DAPT recovers to 0.97--1.0, while MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training (F1\,=\,0.289 $\approx$ vanilla). On the \emph{benign side}, distributional mismatch drives false positives: WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\%, but as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate it (95\% CI upper 6.1\%). RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
21	
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Two-axis generalization decomposition.} We identify two orthogonal generalization challenges in multi-turn jailbreak detection. Attack-side: controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations reveals classification DAPT as the most robust variant (vanilla F1\,=\,0.28--0.31 on OOD $\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0); unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. Benign-side: 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate WildChat FPR from 68.1\% to 0\% (95\% CI upper 6.1\%) without detection degradation.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Condition-dependent adaptation effectiveness and constraining negative results.} MLM is effective on single-source data but collapses under mixed-LLM training; classification DAPT is robust across conditions. Three supplementary negative results constrain which properties matter: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels on template-based attacks (TOST-confirmed on DD and FITD at $n$\,=\,10, $d$\,=\,0.44 on structurally complex ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant ($\Delta < 1$\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.
27	
28	  \item \textbf{Practical deployment recipe.} Continued pretraining is 24.3$\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), and 10-shot benign adaptation eliminates domain-specific false positives. No target-domain attack data is required for deployment.
29	\end{itemize}
30	
```

R13 审稿引用了 Appendix O Table 18 数据：vanilla WildChat FPR=48.1% vs 9-class FPR=68.1%。DAPT 使 FPR 增加 20pp。在 Discussion 的 benign bottleneck 段落中增加一句话明确披露："Classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1% (vanilla) to 68.1%, indicating that adaptation amplifies benign distribution sensitivity while improving attack-side generalization."

### 5. Q2: Task-structure-specific 解释
Discussion 的 mechanism 段落（已从 Contribution 降级为假说后），增加一段解释为什么 9-class 在 ToxicChat 上弱于 MLM：
- Classification DAPT 优化的是 multi-turn representation structure，不是 universal regularization
- 在匹配条件下（multi-turn synthetic OOD）有效，在不匹配条件下（single-turn human data）无效
- MLM 保留通用语言表征，对 domain shift 更鲁棒
- 措辞建议："Classification DAPT's advantage appears specific to the multi-turn synthetic domain where training and evaluation structures match"

### 6. W4: Cross-architecture reframe
- "Cross-encoder validation" → "Cross-architecture observation"
- 明确承认 architecture confound（DeBERTa-v3 vs RoBERTa 不仅是 scale 差异）
- 讨论 RoBERTa-large vanilla AA F1=1.000 但 DAPT 降至 0.946：对足够强的预训练模型，DAPT 可能不必要甚至有微小负面影响。标注为 methodology scope limitation。

### 7. W5: Mixed-LLM caveat
intro 或 experiments 中引用 mixed-LLM 结果时，不将 ActorAttack 0.504±0.107 作为正面结果呈现。加 caveat "remains challenging on structurally complex attacks (ActorAttack F1=0.504)"。

### 8. W6: FITD disclaimer
Section 4.2（或对应的 cross-attack evaluation section）开头加一句："FITD serves as a near-ceiling baseline where all variants achieve F1≥0.990, providing limited discriminative power."

### 9. Q3: Limitations 补充
Limitations 增加一条：benign adaptation 的 10-shot 结果仅在 WildChat (GPT-3.5 conversations) 上验证，跨平台（如 Claude, enterprise chat systems）泛化未评估。

### 10. MR1-5: 缺失引用
- **MR1 (critical)**: Maennel et al. 2020 "What Do Neural Networks Learn When Trained with Random Labels?" — 连接到 scrambled label discussion
- **MR2**: JailDAM (2025) — multi-turn jailbreak detection
- **MR3**: Anil et al. (2024) "Many-shot Jailbreaking"
- **MR4**: Jain et al. (2023) "Baseline Defenses for Adversarial Attacks Against Aligned Language Models"
- **MR5**: Activation-based jailbreak detection methods（搜索近期相关论文）
在 related work 或 discussion 中适当引用。bib 条目需要真实完整信息——如果不确定，用 WebSearch 确认。

### 11. VL1: Table 字号
Appendix Tables 7, 8 字号 ≤6pt，打印可读性差。如果可以增大字号则调整；如果空间不允许，保持现状（appendix 不受 8 页限制）。

    "oldString": "\\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection)",
    "newString": "\\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Pattern Detection)",
    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants systematically vary training objectives while sharing the same two-stage pipeline (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions reveals two orthogonal generalization axes. On the \\emph{attack side}, vanilla DeBERTa drops to F1\\,=\\,0.28--0.31 on OOD attacks; classification DAPT recovers to 0.97--1.0, while MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla). On the \\emph{benign side}, distributional mismatch drives false positives: WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\\%, but as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate it (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%). RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Two-axis generalization decomposition.} We identify two orthogonal generalization challenges in multi-turn jailbreak detection. Attack-side: controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations reveals classification DAPT as the most robust variant (vanilla F1\\,=\\,0.28--0.31 on OOD $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0); unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. Benign-side: 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate WildChat FPR from 68.1\\% to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Condition-dependent adaptation effectiveness and constraining negative results.} MLM is effective on single-source data but collapses under mixed-LLM training; classification DAPT is robust across conditions. Three supplementary negative results constrain which properties matter: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels on template-based attacks (TOST-confirmed on DD and FITD at $n$\\,=\\,10, $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on structurally complex ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant ($\\Delta < 1$\\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Practical deployment recipe.} Continued pretraining is 24.3$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), and 10-shot benign adaptation eliminates domain-specific false positives. No target-domain attack data is required for deployment.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.",
          " ",

DAPT 把表征空间压缩到 1-4 维有效维度，而 MLM 保持 7 维，vanilla 13 维\"}}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

### Assistant

_source line 2157 · 2026-05-22T07:33:01.762Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent results from exp49! Let me analyze:

1. **Weight Distance**: All variants have similar weight distance from pretrained (~0.002-0.004 relative norm). Classification variants are actually slightly LARGER, not smaller. This doesn't directly support "regularization" in the sense of less change.

2. **SVD Geometry**: Classification DAPT causes representation COLLAPSE - far fewer effective dimensions (PR 3.73-9.37) vs MLM (24-25) vs vanilla (31.93). This is very interesting.

3. **Clustering**: Classification DAPT produces massively higher Fisher ratio (binary 19.73, 9-class 4.47, scrambled 2.60) vs MLM/vanilla (~0). 

So classification training isn't regularizing in the traditional sense—it's actually restructuring the representation space by compressing it into fewer dimensions while dramatically improving class separation. The scrambled label result is particularly revealing: even with incorrect labels, the classification objective still produces high Fisher ratios and compressed representations, suggesting the discriminative structure comes from the training objective itself rather than label quality.

Now I need to update the registry with the exp49 results, register the artifact, coordinate with the P1 and MR Workers before tackling the mechanism paragraph rewrite, and then report back to the Director.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 2158 · 2026-05-22T07:33:03.748Z_

exp49 结果非常有价值。关键发现：

- **分类 DAPT 产生 representation collapse**（PR 3.73-9.37 vs vanilla 31.93）
- **Fisher ratio 高 3-5 个数量级**（binary 19.73, 9-class 4.47 vs vanilla 0.00007）
- **Weight distance 相近**（所有变体 ~0.2-0.4% relative），但表征结构差异巨大
- **Scrambled 也有高 Fisher ratio**（2.60），支持"分类结构本身驱动判别性"

这意味着机制不是传统意义的"regularization"（更少改变），而是**discriminative compression**。更新 registry。

{
  "text": "[资源] GPU 0/0 | Worker 2 (GPU 0 / CPU 2) | westd-33183 cuda:0 空闲\n\n## exp49_representation_analysis — SUCCESS ✅\n\n### 核心发现\n\n**Hypothesis 部分证实，但需要修正机制描述。**\n\n原假设：分类 DAPT 产生更小的 weight perturbation 和更均匀的表征。\n实际：weight distance 相近（所有变体 ~0.2-0.4% relative），但**表征结构差异巨大**。\n\n#### A1 Weight Distance\n所有变体改动幅度相近。分类变体（3.16-4.00）反而略大于 MLM（2.92-3.38）。**不支持** \"更少改变\" 的 regularization 故事。\n\n#### A2 SVD Geometry（最关键发现）\n| 类型 | Participation Ratio | 有效维度 |\n|------|-------------------|---------|\n| Vanilla | 31.93 | 13 |\n| MLM (wiki/JB) | 24-25 | 7 |\n| Classification (9c/scr/bin) | **3.73-9.37** | **1-3** |\n\n**分类 DAPT 产生 representation collapse**——将高维表征压缩到极少数维度。\n\n#### A3 Fisher Ratio（类别可分性）\n| 类型 | Fisher Ratio |\n|------|-------------|\n| Vanilla | 0.00007 |\n| MLM | 0.0003-0.0008 |\n| Classification | **2.60-19.73** |\n\n分类变体的 Fisher ratio 比 MLM/vanilla 高 **3-5 个数量级**。Scrambled 也有高 Fisher ratio（2.60），因为分类结构本身（无论标签正确性）迫使模型学习判别边界。\n\n### 对机制描述的启示\n\n\"Optimization regularization\" 不准确。准确描述是 **discriminative compression**：\n- 分类训练压缩表征到少数判别维度（PR 下降 4-9×）\n- 同时最大化类别可分性（Fisher ratio 上升 3-5 OOM）\n- 这解释了为什么 scrambled 也有效：分类结构（不是标签语义）驱动压缩\n- 也解释了为什么 frozen probing 接近端到端：判别结构已经在 Stage-1 建立\n\n**建议 B3 措辞方向**：\n- \"optimization regularization\" → \"discriminative compression\" 或 \"classification-induced representation restructuring\"\n- 引用 SVD/Fisher 数据作为直接证据\n- 保留 \"classification structure matters more than label content\" 的核心论点（scrambled Fisher=2.60 支持）\n\n### 待处理\n- P1 写作 Worker + MR 引用 Worker 仍在运行\n- 两者完成后，我做 B3 mechanism 段落最终修改（基于 exp49 数据）",
  "project": "persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect"
}

```text
0	% experiments.tex — D024 compressed for 8-page limit
1	
2	\section{Experiments}
3	
4	\paragraph{Terminology.}
5	\emph{Cross-attack OOD}: evaluation on attack families unseen during training; benign distribution remains IID throughout (OOD reflects attack-side shift only). $K$: prefix length (number of conversation turns retained). \emph{DAPT}: all Stage-1 adaptations, extending \citet{gururangan2020dontstop} to supervised variants. \emph{Evidence hierarchy}: vanilla $<$ MLM $<$ classification (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}).\footnote{Throughout, OOD refers to attack-family shift (novel persuasion strategies at test time); benign distribution shift is evaluated in Section~\ref{sec:discussion}.}
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}
8	
9	\paragraph{Data.}
10	All synthetic data is generated by Qwen3-8B~\citep{qwen3_2025}.
11	The IID dataset contains 250 Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} jailbreak and 250 topic-anchored benign conversations, split into training (350), validation (74), and test (76) sets with balanced class ratios (e.g., test = 38 jailbreak + 38 benign).
12	Stage~1 (DAPT) uses training + validation conversations; Stage~2 (BiGRU) trains on training, early-stops on validation, and evaluates on test.
13	OOD evaluation pairs attack-specific jailbreak conversations with the 38 IID-test benign conversations: 80 FITD~\citep{fitd2024} (non-discriminative; Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}) + 38 benign = 118 total, 80 Deceptive Delight (DD)~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} + 38 benign = 118 total, and 80 ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} + 38 benign = 118 total.
14	No overlap exists in attack templates across families; benign is IID (same generation pipeline).
15	A paraphrased version of the IID test set (BERTScore 0.92, lexical overlap 0.20) serves as the adversarial robustness benchmark (Section~\ref{sec:perturbation}).
16	For human data validation, we use ToxicChat~\citep{lin2023toxicchat} (204 jailbreak + 204 benign from real LMSYS user interactions), entirely independent of Qwen3-8B generation.
17	
18	\paragraph{Models.}
19	Seven DeBERTa-v3 variants share the same Stage-2 pipeline and differ only in Stage-1 training (Section~\ref{sec:method}).
20	Our vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU configuration is architecturally equivalent to DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026}, differing only in encoder choice (DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ BERT); it serves as our primary baseline.
21	For cross-architecture comparison, we replicate vanilla, MLM, and 9-class variants on RoBERTa-base~\citep{liu2019roberta} (Section~\ref{sec:cross_encoder}).
22	We also compare TF-IDF LR, LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}), and Llama Guard~3~8B~\citep{metallamaguard3_2024} (zero-shot; evaluates last-response safety given conversation context), which detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).
23	
24	\paragraph{Metrics.}
25	F1 for binary jailbreak detection at prefix $K$=2 and full conversations (full per-$K$ results in Appendix~\ref{sec:full_prefix}).
26	OOD results report 3-seed mean$\pm$std (seeds 42, 123, 456) for all variants; TOST equivalence tests for 9-class vs.\ scrambled use 5 seeds (42, 123, 456, 789, 1000).
27	For key pairwise comparisons, we report BCa bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (10,000 resamples over individual predictions pooled across 3 seeds; this assumes cross-seed independence and is exploratory) and paired permutation tests (10,000 permutations). Seed-level F1 values (Table~\ref{tab:seed_level}, Appendix~\ref{app:seed_level}) confirm all pairwise conclusions hold at every seed.
28	Unless otherwise noted, all standard deviations are computed over 3 random seeds using the population formula ($n$ divisor); Bessel-corrected estimates ($n{-}1$ divisor) are $\approx$22\% larger but do not alter any significance conclusions.
29	Stage-1: learning rate $2 \times 10^{-5}$, batch 16, 4 epochs, MLM masking 15\%.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01SQCqpTZkaojBW7aNLnDSaU`</summary>

```text
1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97).
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level. A mixed-source training pilot preserves Qwen OOD detection with preliminary Llama transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}).
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance and capacity.}
13	Classification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD).
14	
15	\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}
16	Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp), attack-dependent label sensitivity ($d \approx 0$ on DD/FITD, $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, paralleling self-supervised findings where training structure matters more than label content~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. Random-label \emph{pretraining} produces transferable representations~\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, confirming that classification structure regularizes the space. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\ benign-side.}
19	OOD F1 throughout this paper reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\% (vanilla) to 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Cross-LLM error decomposition attributes collapse to false positives on unfamiliar benign text. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\% (95\% CI upper 6.1\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}).
20	
21	\section{Conclusion}
22	
23	Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via optimization regularization; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\%\,$\rightarrow$\,0\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.
24	
25	\section{Limitations}
26	\label{sec:limitations}
27	
28	All multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}).
29	
30	OOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\,=\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.
31	
32	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.
33	
34	\section*{Ethics Statement}
35	
36	This work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.
37	
```

Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nControlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via optimization regularization; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\\%\\,$\\rightarrow$\\,0\\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nAll multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\nOOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",
      "numLines": 37,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 37
    }

\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}
Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp), attack-dependent label sensitivity ($d \approx 0$ on DD/FITD, $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, paralleling self-supervised findings where training structure matters more than label content~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. 

Here is the next thinking you need to rewrite:
Random-label \emph{pretraining} produces transferable representations~\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, confirming that classification structure regularizes the space. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
```

I need to replace "optimization regularization" with evidence-backed "discriminative compression" or similar based on exp49 data. Key data points:
- Classification DAPT: PR 3.73-9.37 (vs vanilla 31.93, MLM 24-25) —

The exp49 data shows classification DAPT achieves dramatically lower perturbation robustness (3.73-9.37) compared to vanilla and MLM baselines, suggesting the discriminative structure compresses the representation space in a way that constrains adversarial directions. Random-label pretraining confirms this isn't about label semantics but rather the classification objective itself imposing geometric constraints. However, this compression benefit is context-dependent: it dominates in multi-turn synthetic settings but MLM's broader regularization generalizes better on single-turn human data like ToxicChat.

I should update the conclusion and abstract to frame this as discriminative compression rather than optimization regularization, and search the introduction for similar language that needs revision.

    "originalFile": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97).\n\n\\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}\nToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 (+53\\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level. A mixed-source training pilot preserves Qwen OOD detection with preliminary Llama transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}).\n\n\\paragraph{Variance and capacity.}\nClassification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).\n\n\\paragraph{Regularization mechanism.}\nFrozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\\,pp), attack-dependent label sensitivity ($d \\approx 0$ on DD/FITD, $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on ActorAttack), and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) converge: classification DAPT provides robustness through optimization regularization, paralleling self-supervised findings where training structure matters more than label content~\\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol}. Random-label \\emph{pretraining} produces transferable representations~\\citep{zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}, confirming that classification structure regularizes the space. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\n\\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\\ benign-side.}\nOOD F1 throughout this paper reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\\% (vanilla) to 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Cross-LLM error decomposition attributes collapse to false positives on unfamiliar benign text. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nControlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via optimization regularization; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\\%\\,$\\rightarrow$\\,0\\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nAll multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\nOOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01GZ8DXFgVgr1A7UHEwfdQYQ`</summary>

```text
1	% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Detecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.
4	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, evading existing defenses: Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\% of multi-turn jailbreaks.
5	We isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.
6	On single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\,=\,0.818 vs.\ 0.291 without adaptation).
7	Across three architectures (125--355M), attacks requiring adaptation shrink with scale, though architecture differences prevent isolating scale alone.
8	Classification supervision reduces prediction variance on synthetic data with attack-complexity-dependent label sensitivity, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.
9	Orthogonal to attack-side generalization, benign distribution shift is a practical bottleneck: WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\%, but as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate false positives (95\% CI upper 6.1\%).
10	These findings decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (solved by classification DAPT through optimization regularization) and benign-side (solved by few-shot adaptation), with 24.3$\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01BYrZzcG3xx7LFqGvthXNQx`</summary>

```text
15	\paragraph{Mechanism: discriminative compression.}
16	Representation analysis reveals the mechanism: classification DAPT compresses the feature space into few discriminative dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\ 25 for MLM and 32 for vanilla) while increasing class separability by 3--5 orders of magnitude (Fisher ratio 2.6--19.7 vs.\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\ref{app:representation}). Weight distances from pretrained are similar across all variants ($\sim$0.2--0.4\% relative), so the effect is representational restructuring, not parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce comparable compression (Fisher\,=\,2.6), confirming that classification structure---not label content---drives the effect~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol,zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}. Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp) and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) corroborate: discriminative structure is established in Stage-1, not Stage-2. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
17	
```

700	AA OOD & 50.1\% & 96.2\% & 1 / 7 / 1 & $\sim$0.24 \\
701	\bottomrule
702	\end{tabular}
703	\end{table}
704	
705	Table~\ref{tab:strategy_pred} reports OOD strategy prediction accuracy alongside detection rates. Strategy prediction accuracy is substantially above random (50.1\% vs.\ 11.1\% chance for 9 classes) and positively correlated with detection success ($r \approx 0.24$--0.45), but strategy prediction is not the detection mechanism: frozen probing with a randomly initialized strategy head (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}) achieves 97.2\% AA OOD detection, and $\lambda$=0 (no strategy loss) matches or exceeds $\lambda > 0$ during E2E fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:lambda}). The classification objective shapes representations during Stage-1 DAPT training; at inference, strategy predictions are a byproduct of those representations, not a driver of detection.
706	
707	\section{Cross-Seed Ensemble}
708	\label{app:ensemble}
709	
710	\begin{table}[ht]
711	\centering
712	\footnotesize
713	\caption{Majority vote ensemble (3 seeds) vs.\ single-seed mean on OOD Full. ``Fixed'' = samples corrected by ensemble; ``Broke'' = samples corrupted by ensemble.}
714	\label{tab:ensemble}
715	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
716	\toprule
717	\textbf{Variant} & DD single$\to$ens. & AA single$\to$ens. & Fixed & Broke \\
718	\midrule
719	9-class & .997$\to$\textbf{1.000} & .972$\to$\textbf{.981} & 5 & 0 \\
720	MLM & .914$\to$\textbf{1.000} & .897$\to$\textbf{.953} & 26 & 0 \\
721	Vanilla & .258$\to$.258 & .258$\to$.258 & 0 & 0 \\
722	\bottomrule
723	\end{tabular}
724	\end{table}
725	
726	Table~\ref{tab:ensemble} shows that majority vote never corrupts correct predictions (Broke\,=\,0 for all variants). MLM benefits most from ensembling (+8.6\,pp DD, +6.0\,pp AA), because its per-seed errors are complementary---each seed fails on different samples, and the majority corrects these. This further exposes MLM's seed instability: its individual seeds are unreliable, but their errors are uncorrelated. By contrast, 9-class seeds are already highly consistent (std\,$\leq$\,0.026), leaving minimal room for ensemble improvement (+0.3--0.9\,pp). Vanilla errors are systematic (all seeds fail on the same OOD samples), so ensembling provides no benefit.
727	
728	\section{Seed-Level F1 Comparisons}
729	\label{app:seed_level}

  "new_string": "\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Attack} & \\textbf{Variant} & s42 & s123 & s456 & s789 & s1000 & Mean$\\pm$SD \\\\\n\\midrule\nDD & 9-class   & 1.000 & 1.000 & .990 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .998$\\pm$.004 \\\\\nDD & Scrambled  & .990 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .998$\\pm$.004 \\\\\n\\midrule\nAA & 9-class   & .990 & .935 & .990 & .972 & .972 & .972$\\pm$.021 \\\\\nAA & Scrambled  & .891 & .990 & .971 & .990 & .990 & .966$\\pm$.040 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Representation Analysis}\n\\label{app:representation}\n\nTo characterize the mechanism behind classification DAPT's robustness, we analyze Stage-1 representations across all seven DeBERTa-v3-base variants (seed 42). We extract \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings from 150 test conversations (50 IID + 50 DD OOD + 50 AA OOD) and compute three metrics.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Representation geometry and class separability across Stage-1 variants. PR: participation ratio (effective dimensionality of the embedding space). Fisher ratio: inter-class distance$^2$ / sum of intra-class variances. Weight $\\Delta$: L2 distance from pretrained weights (relative to pretrained norm).}\n\\label{tab:representation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Variant} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{Fisher} & \\textbf{Wt.\\ $\\Delta$ (\\%)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & 31.9 & $<$0.001 & 0.000 \\\\\nWiki-MLM & 25.0 & $<$0.001 & 0.221 \\\\\nJB-MLM & 24.6 & $<$0.001 & 0.256 \\\\\nTopic & 14.5 & 0.012 & 0.371 \\\\\nScrambled & 7.8 & 2.60 & 0.283 \\\\\n9-class & 9.4 & 4.47 & 0.303 \\\\\nBinary & 3.7 & 19.7 & 0.239 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nClassification variants compress representations into 2--9 effective dimensions (vs.\\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing Fisher ratio by 3--5 orders of magnitude. Weight distances from pretrained are comparable across all variants ($\\sim$0.2--0.4\\%), indicating that the mechanism is representational restructuring rather than parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce similar compression (PR\\,=\\,7.8, Fisher\\,=\\,2.6), confirming that the classification objective structure---not label semantics---drives discriminative compression."

Population std convention (2026-05-18)\\n- 全文统一 population std（除以 N），不用 sample std（除以 N-1）。\\n\\n### D036: Drop verazuo, ToxicChat only (2026-05-19)\\n- **决策**：verazuo human data 因 benign data leakage（78% from train split）永久弃用。人类数据评估仅用 ToxicChat（无 leakage）。\\n- **原因**：GRU 训练时可能见过 verazuo benign embedding → data leakage\\n\\n### D037: Claim 3 scope 限定 (2026-05-19)\\n- perturbation 实验移至 Appendix，cross-attack 限定为 synthetic。\\n\\n### D038: 实验阶段结束 (2026-05-19)\\n- 当前证据足够，不再需要更多实验。进入论文修改收尾阶段。DDL 2026-05-25。\\n\\n## 实验经验\\n\\n### 磁盘空间约束 (2026-05-17, updated 2026-05-22)\\n- westd-33183 根分区总容量 30GB。DeBERTa checkpoint ~735MB。\\n- 旧 checkpoint（exp1/mf1/plan 系列）已迁移至 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints_archive/`\\n- 新实验 checkpoint 输出到 `/root/autodl-tmp/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/checkpoints/`\\n- 2026-05-22: 清理 19 个旧 checkpoint 目录释放 ~19.6GB，当前约 21GB 可用\\n\\n### ⚠️ Single-seed 结论偏差警告 (2026-05-18)\\n- GRU 随机性可导致 single-seed 偏差 25pp+，必须多 seed 验证\\n\\n### ⚠️ 跨 LLM 泛化：攻击模式可迁移，benign 分布是瓶颈 (2026-05-19, exp14+exp14b+exp14c)\\n- **exp14 (Llama all)**：所有 variant 崩溃至随机水平（9class 0.433, jb_mlm 0.282, vanilla 0.261）\\n- **exp14b (Llama JB + Qwen benign)**：9class 回升至 1.000 (+56.7pp), jb_mlm 回升至 0.778 (+49.6pp)\\n- **exp14c (confusion matrix)**：TP rate 完全一致（exp14 vs exp14b），FP rate Llama benign 88-100% vs Qwen benign 0%\\n- **结论**：DAPT 学到的攻击模式跨 LLM 可迁移，但 benign 分布覆盖不足导致高 FP。F1 崩溃 100% 来自 FP 爆炸\\n- **论文定位**：limitation 归为 \\\"benign distribution coverage\\\"（可解决的数据多样性问题），而非方法论缺陷\\n- **Future work**：多 LLM benign 数据扩增\\n\\n### ⚠️ Capacity-DAPT 交互效应 (2026-05-19, exp15+exp15b+exp15c)\\n- **exp15 发现**：RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla AA Full=1.000 > 9class 0.946。大模型容量足以直接解决 AA\\n- **exp15b 证伪 catastrophic forgetting**：LR=1e-5 两个数据集均更差（DD 0.948 vs 0.987, AA 0.781 vs 0.946）。不是超参问题\\n- **exp15c MLM-only DAPT 也 hurt**：MLM AA Full=0.874，比 9class 0.946 和 vanilla 1.000 都差。不是 classification head 的问题\\n- **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\\n\\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\\n\\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\\n\\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + RoBERTa-large）GRU 参数一致：hidden_dim=256, num_layers=2, MLP 512→256→2\\n\\n### ⚠️ MLM checkpoint 格式不兼容 (2026-05-19, exp16+exp17)\\n- **问题**: `train_deberta_mlm.py` 用 HF `save_pretrained()` 保存为 safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\\\"model.pt\\\")` 加载\\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 `AutoModel.from_pretrained()` 原生支持 safetensors\\n- **教训**: exp16 和 exp17 MLM 均因此崩溃，每次都需手动修复。应修改 train_classifier.py 的 treatment mode 统一支持 safetensors\\n\\n### ⚠️ exp16 Multi-LLM 结果 (2026-05-19)\\n- **无数据泄漏**: conversation_id 和内容 MD5 均无 train/test 重叠\\n- **9-class F1=1.0 on mixed_test 是真实的**: 76 样本小测试集 + 强 DAPT embedding，BiGRU Epoch 1 即达 val_acc 0.9865\\n- **Train Loss=0.0 是过拟合不是泄漏**: BiGRU 在预计算 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 9-class，dd_ood .422, aa_ood .217, llama_aa .124\\n\\n### ⚠️ Frozen probing: DAPT 类型间表征质量差异小 (2026-05-20, exp26+exp36)\\n- **发现**：冻结 9-class (exp26) vs 冻结 MLM (exp36) 的 probing 性能差距仅 3.1pp AA OOD (0.972 vs 0.941)\\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length shortcut\\n- **机制**：AA 攻击信号更微妙（no explicit harmful content），大量 benign 尾部 turns 稀释 BiGRU attention，导致漏检\\n- **论文定位**：Part 1+2 正面回应 Reviewer 的 length confound 质疑；Part 3 AA vulnerability 作为 Limitation 披露，future work 方向为 attention-aware temporal modeling\\n\\n### ⚠️ TOST 10-seed: Label correctness IS family-dependent (2026-05-22, exp41)\\n- **发现**：扩展到 n=10 seeds 后，DD/FITD 仍确认 label equivalence（TOST p≈0），但 AA 上非等价性更明显（p=0.183, d=0.44 medium effect）\\n- d 从 n=5 的 0.09 升至 n=10 的 0.44——n=5 时的\\\"negligible\\\"是统计效力不足的假象\\n- 9-class AA mean=0.979, scrambled AA mean=0.949, gap=3pp，但 scrambled 方差大（seed 314: 0.833 outlier）\\n- **论文已更新**：所有 5 处主文 + 3 处 appendix 已按 Director Option A 修改。\\\"optimization regularization\\\" framing 加了 \\\"primarily\\\" 和 \\\"with residual semantic contribution on structurally complex attacks\\\"\\n\\n### ✅ Benign distribution fix: 150 WildChat eliminates FPR (2026-05-22, exp42)\\n- **发现**：混入仅 150 条 WildChat benign（30% of training set）即将 WildChat FPR 从 68.1% 降至 0%\\n- OOD 检测性能零退化：DD=1.0, AA=0.956, FITD=1.0\\n- 评估集仅 49 条（226 中 27 条疑似 jailbreak 被过滤），小样本但信号极强\\n- **与 exp38 对比**：retraining with diverse benign >> threshold calibration（exp38 calibration 只能 79%→63% FPR）\\n- **论文影响**：等 Director 决策是否更新 Discussion/Limitations 的 FPR 描述\\n\\n### ✅ Benign adaptation curve: 10-shot 即消除 FPR (2026-05-22, exp45)\\n- **发现**：7-point curve N∈{0,5,10,25,50,100,150}，FPR 65.3%→16.3%→0% at N=10\\n- N=10 后所有 OOD F1 ≥ 0.953，无检测退化\\n- 95% CI upper 6.1%（n=49 WildChat eval）\\n- **论文已更新**：Discussion + Limitations + Appendix（含完整 7-point 表格）\\n\\n### ❌ Ensemble JB-MLM + 9-class 无 Pareto 改进 (2026-05-22, exp48)\\n- **发现**：3 种融合方法（logit avg, vote AND/OR, weighted avg）均无法同时超越 JB-MLM 单独性能\\n- JB-MLM alone: TC F1=0.820, WC FPR=30.6%；9-class alone: TC F1=0.596, WC FPR=75.5%\\n- Vote AND 降低 FPR（18.4%）但牺牲 TC F1（0.735）和 AA F1（0.962）\\n- **原因**：9-class 在 human data 和 benign 判别上弱，error pattern 不互补而是 9-class 弱点主导融合输出\\n- **教训**：不同 DAPT 目标产生的模型弱点是相关的（都在 benign **结论**：continued pretraining 本身的 representation drift 与大模型冲突。任何形式的 DAPT 在 AA 上都 hurt 355M 模型\\n- **但 DD OOD 上 9class 仍有价值**：9class 0.987 > vanilla 0.927 > MLM 0.884。Classification DAPT 在难任务上的定向表征重塑仍有边际价值\\n- **完整 RoBERTa-large Table 3**：\\n\\n| | DD OOD Full | AA Full |\\n|--|:-----------:|:------:|\\n| Vanilla | 0.927 | **1.000** |\\n| 9class | **0.987** | 0.946 |\\n| MLM | 0.884 | 0.874 |\\n\\n- **论文定位**：Discussion 中 capacity-drift tradeoff——小模型需要 DAPT 弥补 capacity 不足，大模型 capacity 已够但 DAPT 引入 drift 反而有害\\n\\n### GRU 架构一致性审计 (2026-05-19)\\n- 所有实验（DeBERTa + RoBERTa-base + safetensors，但 `train_classifier.py` 的 treatment mode 用 `torch.load(\\\"model.pt\\\")` 加载\\n- **修复**: MLM Stage 2 改用 `--mode baseline --model_name &lt;mlm_checkpoint_path>`，通过 embedding 上 Epoch 2 即完全记忆训练集，best model 保存在 Epoch 1\\n- **MLM 在 mixed-LLM 下高度不稳定**: seed 123 异常（全预测 jailbreak），std 0.2-0.5\\n- **证据层级成立**: 9-class >> vanilla，但 MLM 不稳定（均值低于 vanilla）\\n- **OOD 仍弱**: 即使 (0.972 vs 0.941)\\n- **两种 DAPT 都远超 vanilla** (exp20: DD=0.318, AA=0.275)\\n- **结论**：DAPT 的核心贡献在于 frozen representation 质量提升（vs vanilla 飞跃），classification vs MLM DAPT 的差异主要体现在 E2E fine-tuning 阶段的训练稳定性（9-class std 更小），而非 frozen 表征质量\\n- **论文定位**：C1 mechanism 细化为两层——(1) DAPT vs no-DAPT = 表征质量飞跃 (2) classification vs MLM = E2E 稳定性差异\\n\\n### ⚠️ BiGRU attention dilution 漏洞 (2026-05-21, exp37)\\n- **发现**：Jailbreak 对话追加 benign padding 到 15+ turns 后，DD 检测率仅降 2.5pp（0.996→0.971），但 AA 暴跌 57.9pp（0.963→0.383）\\n- **同时**：Benign 截断到 3 turns 后 FPR 仍为 0；WildChat 短对话 FPR 反而低于长对话 → 模型不依赖 length 314: 0.833 outlier）\\n- **论文已更新**：所有 5 处主文 + 3 处 appendix 已按 Director Option A 修改。\\\"optimization regularization\\\" framing 加了 \\\"primarily\\\" 和 \\\"with residual semantic contribution on FPR）\\n- **论文影响**：等 Director 决策是否更新 Discussion/Limitations 的 FPR 描述\\n\\n### ✅ Benign adaptation curve: 10-shot 即消除 FPR (2026-05-22, exp45)\\n- **发现**：7-point curve N∈{0,5,10,25,50,100,150}，FPR adaptation curve: 10-shot 即消除 FPR (2026-05-22, exp45)\n- **发现**：7-point curve N∈{0,5,10,25,50,100,150}，FPR 65.3%→16.3%→0% at N=10\n- N=10 后所有 OOD F1 ≥ 0.953，无检测退化\n- 95% CI upper 6.1%（n=49 WildChat eval）\n- **论文已更新**：Discussion + Limitations + Appendix（含完整 7-point 表格）\n\n### ❌ Ensemble JB-MLM + 9-class 无 Pareto 改进 (2026-05-22, exp48)\n- **发现**：3 种融合方法均无法同时超越 JB-MLM 单独性能\n- **教训**：不同 DAPT 目标产生的模型弱点是相关的，ensemble 不能解决共同弱点\n\n### ✅ Representation Analysis: 机制是 discriminative compression (2026-05-22, exp49)\n- **发现**：分类 DAPT 将表征空间压缩到极少数判别维度\n  - PR（有效维度）：binary 3.7, scrambled 7.8, 9-class 9.4 vs MLM 24-25 vs vanilla 31.9\n  - Fisher ratio：binary 19.7, 9-class 4.5, scrambled 2.6 vs MLM/vanilla <0.001（差 3-5 个数量级）\n  - Weight distance 全部变体相近（~0.2-0.4% relative）\n- **关键推论**：机制不是 \"optimization regularization\"（参数改动量相近），而是 representational restructuring——分类目标迫使模型在少数维度上最大化类别可分性\n- **Scrambled 也有高 Fisher ratio (2.6)**：确认分类结构本身（不是标签语义）驱动 compression\n- **论文已更新**：Discussion 段从 \"optimization regularization\" 改为 \"discriminative compression\"，引用 exp49 数据；Appendix 新增 Representation Analysis section + Table\n\n## API Key / 模型版本\n（待记录）\n"
}

775	\label{app:representation}
776	
777	To characterize the mechanism behind classification DAPT's robustness, we analyze Stage-1 representations across all seven DeBERTa-v3-base variants (seed 42). We extract \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings from 150 test conversations (50 IID + 50 DD OOD + 50 AA OOD) and compute three metrics.
778	
779	\begin{table}[ht]
780	\centering
781	\footnotesize
782	\caption{Representation geometry and class separability across Stage-1 variants. PR: participation ratio (effective dimensionality of the embedding space). Fisher ratio: inter-class distance$^2$ / sum of intra-class variances. Weight $\Delta$: L2 distance from pretrained weights (relative to pretrained norm).}
783	\label{tab:representation}
784	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
785	\toprule
786	\textbf{Variant} & \textbf{PR} & \textbf{Fisher} & \textbf{Wt.\ $\Delta$ (\%)} \\
787	\midrule
788	Vanilla & 31.9 & $<$0.001 & 0.000 \\
789	Wiki-MLM & 25.0 & $<$0.001 & 0.221 \\
790	JB-MLM & 24.6 & $<$0.001 & 0.256 \\
791	Topic & 14.5 & 0.012 & 0.371 \\
792	Scrambled & 7.8 & 2.60 & 0.283 \\
793	9-class & 9.4 & 4.47 & 0.303 \\
794	Binary & 3.7 & 19.7 & 0.239 \\
795	\bottomrule
796	\end{tabular}
797	\end{table}
798	
799	Classification variants compress representations into 2--9 effective dimensions (vs.\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing Fisher ratio by 3--5 orders of magnitude. Weight distances from pretrained are comparable across all variants ($\sim$0.2--0.4\%), indicating that the mechanism is representational restructuring rather than parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce similar compression (PR\,=\,7.8, Fisher\,=\,2.6), confirming that the classification objective structure---not label semantics---drives discriminative compression.

497	\section{Frozen Probing Verification}
498	\label{app:frozen_verify}
499	
500	\begin{table}[ht]
501	\centering
502	\footnotesize
503	\caption{Frozen probing vs.\ end-to-end (E2E) fine-tuning (3-seed mean$\pm$std). 9-class: plan\_002 checkpoints; MLM: plan\_017\_mlm\_control (matched training). Frozen probing trains only BiGRU+MLP on frozen DAPT embeddings.}
504	\label{tab:frozen_verify}
505	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
506	\toprule
507	\textbf{Config} & IID & DD OOD & AA OOD & FITD OOD \\
508	\midrule
509	Frozen 9-class & 1.000$\pm$.000 & .997$\pm$.005 & .972$\pm$.026 & 1.000$\pm$.000 \\
510	Frozen MLM & .982$\pm$.016 & .993$\pm$.009 & .941$\pm$.037 & .987$\pm$.009 \\
511	E2E 9-class & 1.000$\pm$.000 & 1.000$\pm$.000 & .997$\pm$.005 & --- \\
512	\bottomrule
513	\end{tabular}
514	\end{table}
515	
516	Table~\ref{tab:frozen_verify} compares frozen probing across DAPT objectives and against end-to-end fine-tuning. On easy OOD (DD, FITD), frozen MLM and frozen 9-class representations perform comparably ($\Delta \leq 1.3$\,pp). On hard OOD (ActorAttack), 9-class retains a modest advantage (0.972 vs.\ 0.941, $\Delta$\,=\,3.1\,pp) with lower variance ($\sigma$\,=\,0.026 vs.\ 0.037). Both far exceed vanilla encoders ($\leq$0.318; Table~\ref{tab:bert_proxy}). The E2E advantage over frozen 9-class is $\leq$2.5\,pp, confirming that Stage-1 representation quality is the primary source of OOD generalization.
517	
518	A striking asymmetry emerges when comparing frozen probing to the full pipeline (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}): MLM frozen representations \emph{outperform} the full MLM pipeline on AA OOD (0.941 vs.\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen exactly matches its full pipeline (0.972 vs.\ 0.972). This indicates that MLM's cross-seed instability originates in Stage-2 BiGRU training rather than Stage-1 DAPT---the MLM encoder produces adequate representations that are then degraded by unstable downstream optimization. Classification DAPT, by contrast, produces representations that remain robust through Stage-2 training, explaining its consistently lower variance (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).
519	
520	This two-level structure---DAPT vs.\ no-DAPT producing a representation quality leap (+70\,pp), classification vs.\ MLM DAPT producing a stability difference---is consistent with two further observations: $\lambda$=0 matches $\lambda > 0$ during E2E fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:lambda}), and mean pooling matches BiGRU under E2E (Table~\ref{tab:temporal}). Once DAPT representations are strong, downstream configuration details have minimal impact.
521

523	\label{app:temporal}
524

518	A striking asymmetry emerges when comparing frozen probing to the full pipeline (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}): MLM frozen representations \emph{outperform} the full MLM pipeline on AA OOD (0.941 vs.\ 0.893), while 9-class frozen exactly matches its full pipeline (0.972 vs.\ 0.972). This indicates that MLM's cross-seed instability originates in Stage-2 BiGRU training rather than Stage-1 DAPT---the MLM encoder produces adequate representations that are then degraded by unstable downstream optimization. Classification DAPT, by contrast, produces representations that remain robust through Stage-2 training, explaining its consistently lower variance (Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}).
519	
520	This two-level structure---DAPT vs.\ no-DAPT producing a representation quality leap (+70\,pp), classification vs.\ MLM DAPT producing a stability difference---is consistent with two further observations: $\lambda$=0 matches $\lambda > 0$ during E2E fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:lambda}), and mean pooling matches BiGRU under E2E (Table~\ref{tab:temporal}). Once DAPT representations are strong, downstream configuration details have minimal impact.
521	
522	\section{Temporal Aggregation Comparison}
523	\label{app:temporal}
524	
525	\begin{table}[ht]

770	\bottomrule
771	\end{tabular}}
772	\end{table}
773	
774	\paragraph{Representation geometry analysis.}
775	\label{app:representation}
776	
777	To characterize the mechanism behind classification DAPT's robustness, we analyze Stage-1 representations across all seven DeBERTa-v3-base variants (seed 42). We extract \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings from 150 test conversations (50 IID + 50 DD OOD + 50 AA OOD) and compute three metrics.
778	
779	\begin{table}[ht]
780	\centering
781	\footnotesize
782	\caption{Representation geometry and class separability across Stage-1 variants. PR: participation ratio (effective dimensionality of the embedding space). Fisher ratio: inter-class distance$^2$ / sum of intra-class variances. Weight $\Delta$: L2 distance from pretrained weights (relative to pretrained norm).}
783	\label{tab:representation}
784	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
785	\toprule
786	\textbf{Variant} & \textbf{PR} & \textbf{Fisher} & \textbf{Wt.\ $\Delta$ (\%)} \\
787	\midrule
788	Vanilla & 31.9 & $<$0.001 & 0.000 \\
789	Wiki-MLM & 25.0 & $<$0.001 & 0.221 \\
790	JB-MLM & 24.6 & $<$0.001 & 0.256 \\
791	Topic & 14.5 & 0.012 & 0.371 \\
792	Scrambled & 7.8 & 2.60 & 0.283 \\
793	9-class & 9.4 & 4.47 & 0.303 \\
794	Binary & 3.7 & 19.7 & 0.239 \\
795	\bottomrule
796	\end{tabular}
797	\end{table}
798	
799	Classification variants compress representations into 2--9 effective dimensions (vs.\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing Fisher ratio by 3--5 orders of magnitude. Weight distances from pretrained are comparable across all variants ($\sim$0.2--0.4\%), indicating that the mechanism is representational restructuring rather than parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce similar compression (PR\,=\,7.8, Fisher\,=\,2.6), confirming that the classification objective structure---not label semantics---drives discriminative compression.
800

  "old_string": "This two-level structure---DAPT vs.\\ no-DAPT producing a representation quality leap (+70\\,pp), classification vs.\\ MLM DAPT producing a stability difference---is consistent with two further observations: $\\lambda$=0 matches $\\lambda > 0$ during E2E fine-tuning (Table~\\ref{tab:lambda}), and mean pooling matches BiGRU under E2E (Table~\\ref{tab:temporal}). Once DAPT representations are strong, downstream configuration details have minimal impact.\n\n\\section{Temporal Aggregation Comparison}",
  "new_string": "This two-level structure---DAPT vs.\\ no-DAPT producing a representation quality leap (+70\\,pp), classification vs.\\ MLM DAPT producing a stability difference---is consistent with two further observations: $\\lambda$=0 matches $\\lambda > 0$ during E2E fine-tuning (Table~\\ref{tab:lambda}), and mean pooling matches BiGRU under E2E (Table~\\ref{tab:temporal}). Once DAPT representations are strong, downstream configuration details have minimal impact.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation geometry analysis.}\n\\label{app:representation}\n\nTo characterize the mechanism behind classification DAPT's robustness, we analyze Stage-1 representations across all seven DeBERTa-v3-base variants (seed 42). We extract \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings from 150 test conversations (50 IID + 50 DD OOD + 50 AA OOD) and compute three metrics.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Representation geometry and class separability across Stage-1 variants. PR: participation ratio (effective dimensionality of the embedding space). Fisher ratio: inter-class distance$^2$ / sum of intra-class variances. Weight $\\Delta$: L2 distance from pretrained weights (relative to pretrained norm).}\n\\label{tab:representation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Variant} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{Fisher} & \\textbf{Wt.\\ $\\Delta$ (\\%)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & 31.9 & $<$0.001 & 0.000 \\\\\nWiki-MLM & 25.0 & $<$0.001 & 0.221 \\\\\nJB-MLM & 24.6 & $<$0.001 & 0.256 \\\\\nTopic & 14.5 & 0.012 & 0.371 \\\\\nScrambled & 7.8 & 2.60 & 0.283 \\\\\n9-class & 9.4 & 4.47 & 0.303 \\\\\nBinary & 3.7 & 19.7 & 0.239 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nClassification variants compress representations into 2--9 effective dimensions (vs.\\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing Fisher ratio by 3--5 orders of magnitude. Weight distances from pretrained are comparable across all variants ($\\sim$0.2--0.4\\%), indicating that the mechanism is representational restructuring rather than parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce similar compression (PR\\,=\\,7.8, Fisher\\,=\\,2.6), confirming that the classification objective structure---not label semantics---drives discriminative compression.\n\n\\section{Temporal Aggregation Comparison}"
}

      "content": "DD & Scrambled  & .990 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & .998$\\pm$.004 \\\\\n\\midrule\nAA & 9-class   & .990 & .935 & .990 & .972 & .972 & .972$\\pm$.021 \\\\\nAA & Scrambled  & .891 & .990 & .971 & .990 & .990 & .966$\\pm$.040 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Representation geometry analysis.}\n\\label{app:representation}\n\nTo characterize the mechanism behind classification DAPT's robustness, we analyze Stage-1 representations across all seven DeBERTa-v3-base variants (seed 42). We extract \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings from 150 test conversations (50 IID + 50 DD OOD + 50 AA OOD) and compute three metrics.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\caption{Representation geometry and class separability across Stage-1 variants. PR: participation ratio (effective dimensionality of the embedding space). Fisher ratio: inter-class distance$^2$ / sum of intra-class variances. Weight $\\Delta$: L2 distance from pretrained weights (relative to pretrained norm).}\n\\label{tab:representation}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Variant} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{Fisher} & \\textbf{Wt.\\ $\\Delta$ (\\%)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nVanilla & 31.9 & $<$0.001 & 0.000 \\\\\nWiki-MLM & 25.0 & $<$0.001 & 0.221 \\\\\nJB-MLM & 24.6 & $<$0.001 & 0.256 \\\\\nTopic & 14.5 & 0.012 & 0.371 \\\\\nScrambled & 7.8 & 2.60 & 0.283 \\\\\n9-class & 9.4 & 4.47 & 0.303 \\\\\nBinary & 3.7 & 19.7 & 0.239 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nClassification variants compress representations into 2--9 effective dimensions (vs.\\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing Fisher ratio by 3--5 orders of magnitude. Weight distances from pretrained are comparable across all variants ($\\sim$0.2--0.4\\%), indicating that the mechanism is representational restructuring rather than parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce similar compression (PR\\,=\\,7.8, Fisher\\,=\\,2.6), confirming that the classification objective structure---not label semantics---drives discriminative compression.\n",

```text
1	% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit
2	
3	\section{Discussion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	
6	\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}
7	Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97).
8	
9	\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}
10	ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 (+53\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level. A mixed-source training pilot preserves Qwen OOD detection with preliminary Llama transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}).
11	
12	\paragraph{Variance and capacity.}
13	Classification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD).
14	
15	\paragraph{Mechanism: discriminative compression.}
16	Representation analysis reveals the mechanism: classification DAPT compresses the feature space into few discriminative dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\ 25 for MLM and 32 for vanilla) while increasing class separability by 3--5 orders of magnitude (Fisher ratio 2.6--19.7 vs.\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\ref{app:representation}). Weight distances from pretrained are similar across all variants ($\sim$0.2--0.4\% relative), so the effect is representational restructuring, not parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce comparable compression (Fisher\,=\,2.6), confirming that classification structure---not label content---drives the effect~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol,zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}. Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp) and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) corroborate: discriminative structure is established in Stage-1, not Stage-2. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\ benign-side.}
19	OOD F1 throughout this paper reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\% (vanilla) to 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Cross-LLM error decomposition attributes collapse to false positives on unfamiliar benign text. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\% (95\% CI upper 6.1\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}).
20	
21	\section{Conclusion}
22	
23	Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via discriminative compression of the feature space; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\%\,$\rightarrow$\,0\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.
24	
25	\section{Limitations}
26	\label{sec:limitations}
27	
28	All multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\ref{app:length_independence}).
29	
30	OOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\,=\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.
31	
32	Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.
33	
34	\section*{Ethics Statement}
35	
36	This work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.
37	
```
