Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).",
```json
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Confounds and transferability.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97). ToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer; mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 while MLM collapses (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}). Classification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}). TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).",
    "originalFile": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Topic shortcut and label equivalence.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97).\n\n\\paragraph{Single-LLM confound.}\nToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer. Mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 (+53\\,pp) while MLM collapses to vanilla level. A mixed-source training pilot preserves Qwen OOD detection with preliminary Llama transfer (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}).\n\n\\paragraph{Variance and capacity.}\nClassification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}). Three encoders (125M--355M) differ in pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed. TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).\n\n\\paragraph{Mechanism: discriminative compression.}\nRepresentation analysis reveals the mechanism: classification DAPT compresses the feature space into few discriminative dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\\ 25 for MLM and 32 for vanilla) while increasing class separability by 3--5 orders of magnitude (Fisher ratio 2.6--19.7 vs.\\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\\ref{app:representation}). Weight distances from pretrained are similar across all variants ($\\sim$0.2--0.4\\% relative), so the effect is representational restructuring, not parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce comparable compression (Fisher\\,=\\,2.6), confirming that classification structure---not label content---drives the effect~\\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol,zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}. Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\\,pp) and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) corroborate: discriminative structure is established in Stage-1, not Stage-2. This advantage is condition-specific: classification DAPT outperforms MLM only under multi-turn synthetic evaluation; on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\n\\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\\ benign-side.}\nOOD F1 throughout this paper reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\\% (vanilla) to 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Cross-LLM error decomposition attributes collapse to false positives on unfamiliar benign text. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nControlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via discriminative compression of the feature space; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\\%\\,$\\rightarrow$\\,0\\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nAll multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\nOOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "% conclusion.tex — compressed for 8-page limit\n\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Confounds and transferability.}\nThree observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\\,=\\,0.541$\\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97). ToxicChat (+18--42\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer; mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\\,=\\,0.818$\\pm$0.096 while MLM collapses (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}). Classification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\\,=\\,0.087 vs.\\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\\ref{app:frozen_verify}). TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\\ 0.961 on DD).\n\n\\paragraph{Mechanism: discriminative compression.}\nClassification DAPT compresses the feature space into few discriminative dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing class separability by 3--5 orders of magnitude (Fisher ratio 2.6--19.7 vs.\\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\\ref{app:representation}). Weight distances are comparable across variants ($\\sim$0.2--0.4\\%), so the effect is representational restructuring, not parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce comparable compression (Fisher\\,=\\,2.6), confirming that classification structure drives the effect~\\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol,zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}. Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\\,pp) and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\\Delta| < 1$\\,pp) corroborate that discriminative structure is established in Stage-1. This advantage is condition-specific: on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\n\\paragraph{Two-axis generalization: attack-side vs.\\ benign-side.}\nOOD F1 throughout this paper reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: classification DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\\% (vanilla) to 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Cross-LLM error decomposition attributes collapse to false positives on unfamiliar benign text. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}).\n\n\\section{Conclusion}\n\nControlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via discriminative compression of the feature space; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\\%\\,$\\rightarrow$\\,0\\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.\n\n\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nAll multi-turn findings derive from synthetic conversations generated by Qwen3-8B (350 training, 76--118 test per family); preliminary mixed-LLM training (Appendix~\\ref{app:mixed_llm}) suggests this limitation is addressable. ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. Whether the evidence hierarchy generalizes to human-authored multi-turn attacks remains the primary open question (length confound analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:length_independence}).\n\nOOD results reflect attack-side generalization only: benign data is IID, so OOD F1 figures are upper bounds (Section~\\ref{sec:discussion}). On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg}, non-persuasive strategies, and non-text modalities are not addressed. Total pipeline cost is under 4 GPU-hours on a single A100.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work is defensive: it improves detection of multi-turn jailbreak attacks rather than developing new attacks. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms. No human subjects were involved; all data will be released under CC BY-NC 4.0.\n",
```

[Director 指令] D073 Phase 1：信息收集（两项并行，完成后一起汇报）

## 任务 A：J-DAPT 论文搜索

WebSearch 找 arXiv:2509.23281 (J-DAPT)，这是 DAPT 用于 jailbreak 检测的直接前驱工作。需要确认：
1. 论文标题、作者、发布时间
2. 方法：是否做了类似我们的受控消融？还是单一 DAPT 应用？
3. 数据：用了哪些攻击类型/数据集？
4. 核心发现：DAPT 效果如何？是否讨论了 label sensitivity / representation mechanism / benign-side FPR？
5. 与我们的关键差异：我们有 7 变体消融 + discriminative compression 机制 + 条件性标签敏感性 + 两轴分解 + 10-shot benign adaptation，他们有什么我们没有的？

同时搜索 R14 提到的其他可能遗漏引用：
- TrajGuard (arXiv:2604.07727)
- SAE jailbreak mitigators (arXiv:2602.12418)
- Tensor decomposition detection (arXiv:2602.11495)
- Alon et al. (2023) perplexity detection
- Phute et al. (2023) LLM Self Defense

每篇给一句话摘要 + 是否需要引用的判断。

## 任务 B：ToxicChat 错误分析

从已有实验数据中分析 ToxicChat 上 9-class vs JB-MLM 的错误类型分解：
1. 找到 9-class 和 JB-MLM 在 ToxicChat 上的预测结果文件（可能在 exp17 或相关实验的输出中）
2. 计算 FP 和 FN 分别的数量和比例
3. 9-class 的 24pp 劣势主要来自 FP 增加还是 FN 增加？
4. 如果有样本级预测，看 9-class 额外错判的样本有什么特征

同时检查：Table 14 中 1000 样本版本的数据来源——新增的 ~650 条训练数据的 topic/attack-type 分布与原始 350 有何不同？这决定 N4 是否需要控制实验。

两项完成后统一汇报。

````json
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：J-DAPT 论文搜索 + 补充引用调研\n\n### 主要任务：J-DAPT (arXiv:2509.23281)\n\n用 WebSearch 搜索 arXiv:2509.23281 或 \"J-DAPT jailbreak detection DAPT\"，获取：\n1. 论文标题、作者、发布时间、venue\n2. 方法：是否做了受控消融（多变体对比）？还是单一 DAPT 应用？\n3. 数据：用了哪些攻击类型/数据集？multi-turn 还是 single-turn？\n4. 核心发现：DAPT 效果如何？\n5. 是否讨论了以下话题：\n   - Label sensitivity / label correctness\n   - Representation mechanism / 为什么 DAPT works\n   - Benign-side FPR / distribution shift\n   - Cross-attack OOD generalization\n6. 与我们的关键差异。我们的贡献：7 变体消融 + discriminative compression 机制发现 + 条件性标签敏感性（TOST） + 两轴分解（attack-side vs benign-side）+ 10-shot benign adaptation + 24.3× speedup。他们有什么我们没有的？\n\n### 补充引用搜索\n\n对以下每篇论文，WebSearch 获取标题、作者、venue、一句话摘要，判断是否需要在我们论文中引用：\n\n1. **TrajGuard** (arXiv:2604.07727) — 搜索 \"TrajGuard arXiv 2604.07727\"\n2. **SAE jailbreak mitigators** (arXiv:2602.12418) — 搜索 \"SAE jailbreak arXiv 2602.12418\"\n3. **Tensor decomposition detection** (arXiv:2602.11495) — 搜索 \"tensor decomposition jailbreak arXiv 2602.11495\"\n4. **Alon & Kamfonas (2023)** — 搜索 \"Alon Kamfonas 2023 perplexity jailbreak detection\"\n5. **Phute et al. (2023) LLM Self Defense** — 搜索 \"Phute LLM Self Defense 2023\"\n\n引用判断标准：\n- 如果是 multi-turn jailbreak detection → 必须引用（直接竞品）\n- 如果是 single-turn defense 但方法论相关（如 representation-based）→ related work 引用\n- 如果是完全不同的方法（如 perplexity filter）→ 可选引用，一句话提及即可\n\n### 产出格式\n\n```\n## J-DAPT 详细分析\n标题：...\n作者：...\n发布：...\n方法：...\n数据：...\n核心发现：...\n与我们的差异：...\n我们的新颖性优势：...\n\n## 补充引用\n| 论文 | 标题 | Venue | 一句话摘要 | 是否引用 | 理由 |\n```\n",
````

````json
  "task_prompt": "## 任务：ToxicChat 错误分析 + Table 14 数据来源检查\n\n### 任务 1：ToxicChat 上 9-class vs JB-MLM 错误类型分解\n\n#### 背景\n在 ToxicChat（human-authored single-turn jailbreak）上，JB-MLM F1=0.820 远高于 9-class F1=0.596。需要分析这 24pp 差距的来源。\n\n#### 步骤\n1. 在服务器 westd-33183 上搜索 ToxicChat 预测结果文件：\n   ```bash\n   find /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/results/ -name \"*toxic*\" -o -name \"*human*\" | head -20\n   find /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/ -name \"*predictions*\" | head -20\n   ```\n   也检查 exp17/exp42/exp48 相关的结果目录。\n\n2. 如果找到样本级预测文件（包含 true label + predicted label），计算：\n   - 9-class: TP, FP, FN, TN\n   - JB-MLM: TP, FP, FN, TN\n   - 9-class 比 JB-MLM 差的部分主要是 FP 增加还是 FN 增加？\n   \n3. 如果没有现成预测文件，检查训练脚本是否保存了预测输出：\n   ```bash\n   grep -r \"prediction\\|pred_label\\|confusion\" /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/scripts/\n   ```\n\n4. 如果实在没有样本级数据，从已知的 F1/precision/recall 指标推算 FP/FN 比例。检查 registry 和 results JSON 中是否有 precision/recall breakdown。\n\n#### 环境\n- 服务器：westd-33183\n- 环境：`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`\n- 项目路径：`/root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/`\n\n### 任务 2：Table 14 数据来源（1000 样本版本）\n\nTable 14 在论文 appendix 中，对应 exp_id 可能是 exp27 或 dataset_scale 相关。\n\n1. 搜索 1000 样本训练数据的来源：\n   ```bash\n   find /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/ -name \"*1000*\" -o -name \"*scale*\" | head -20\n   grep -r \"1000\\|scale\" /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/scripts/ | head -20\n   ```\n\n2. 检查 1000 样本训练集的 topic 和 attack-type 分布：\n   - 原始 350 条（175 jailbreak + 175 benign）的 topic/strategy 分布\n   - 新增 ~650 条的 topic/strategy 分布\n   - 是否有 topic 偏移？\n\n3. 检查数据文件：\n   ```bash\n   ls /root/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/data/\n   # 找到 1000 样本版本的训练数据\n   ```\n\n### 产出格式\n\n```\n## ToxicChat 错误分析\n- 9-class: TP=?, FP=?, FN=?, TN=?\n- JB-MLM: TP=?, FP=?, FN=?, TN=?\n- 差距来源：主要是 FP / FN / 两者？\n- 样本特征（如果有）：...\n\n## Table 14 数据分布\n- 350 版本分布：...\n- 1000 版本分布：...\n- 是否有 topic 偏移：...\n- 是否需要控制实验：...\n```\n",
````

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019Hk43zhsEc3wWnmDk3i8Fh`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"worker_id\": \"worker_research_jdapt_citations_20260522T082145\", \"status\": \"done\", \"task_type\": \"research:jdapt_citations\", \"project\": \"persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect\", \"started_at\": \"2026-05-22T08:21:45.200691+00:00\", \"ended_at\": \"2026-05-22T08:23:52.516903+00:00\", \"setup\": null, \"teardown\": null, \"setup_data\": {}, \"result\": {\"raw\": \"信息收集完毕，以下是完整分析。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## J-DAPT 详细分析\\n\\n**标题：** Preventing Robotic Jailbreaking via Multimodal Domain Adaptation\\n\\n**作者：** Francesco Marchiori, Rohan Sinha, Christopher Agia, Alexander Robey, George J. Pappas, Mauro Conti, Marco Pavone\\n\\n**发布：** 2025-09-27, arXiv preprint (无正式 venue)\\n\\n**方法：**\\n- 多模态框架（文本 + 视觉），通过 attention-based cross-modal fusion 融合文本和视觉嵌入\\n- 使用 CORAL-based domain adaptation + importance-weighted training，将通用越狱数据集对齐到机器人领域\\n- **有受控消融**：multimodal fusion alone vs. domain adaptation alone vs. full pipeline (J-DAPT)，在多个 embedding model 上对比\\n- 不是单纯的\\\"DAPT 应用\\\"，而是多组件消融\\n\\n**数据：**\\n- 评估域：autonomous driving, maritime robotics, quadruped navigation\\n- **Single-turn 攻击**，针对 LLM/VLM 控制的机器人系统\\n- 不涉及 multi-turn persuasion 攻击\\n\\n**核心发现：**\\n- J-DAPT 平均拦截 98.85% 的越狱攻击\\n- 比最快的 LLM detector 快 9.9×\\n- Fusion alone 对部分模型提升大，DA alone 提升有限，full pipeline 最优\\n\\n**关键话题覆盖：**\\n| 话题 | 是否讨论 |\\n|------|---------|\\n| Label sensitivity / label correctness | 否 |\\n| Representation mechanism / 为什么 DAPT works | 否（仅验证有效性，未做机制分析） |\\n| Benign-side FPR / distribution shift | 有限（报告了检测准确率，但未专门分析 benign 端） |\\n| Cross-attack OOD generalization | 否（仅跨域/跨 embedding model，非跨攻击类型 OOD） |\\n\\n**与我们的关键差异：**\\n- J-DAPT 针对 **机器人多模态** 越狱，我们针对 **LLM 文本 multi-turn persuasion** 越狱 — 问题域完全不同\\n- J-DAPT 的 \\\"domain adaptation\\\" 是跨应用域（通用→机器人），我们的 DAPT 是 discriminative fine-tuning\\n- J-DAPT 无 label sensitivity 分析、无 representation mechanism 探究、无 benign adaptation\\n- J-DAPT 有多模态融合消融，但未做我们的 7 变体消融（compression 方向）\\n\\n**我们的新颖性优势（J-DAPT 不具备的）：**\\n- 7 变体消融 + discriminative compression 机制发现\\n- 条件性标签敏感性（TOST）\\n- 两轴分解（attack-side vs benign-side）\\n- 10-shot benign adaptation\\n- 24.3× speedup\\n- Multi-turn persuasion 攻击场景\\n\\n**他们有什么我们没有的：**\\n- 多模态（视觉+文本）融合能力\\n- 跨应用域（driving/maritime/quadruped）的泛化验证\\n- 重要性加权训练策略\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 补充引用\\n\\n| 论文 | 标题 | Venue | 一句话摘要 | 是否引用 | 理由 |\\n|------|------|-------|-----------|---------|------|\\n| TrajGuard (2604.07727) | TrajGuard: Streaming Hidden-state Trajectory Detection for Decoding-time Jailbreak Defense | arXiv 2026-04 preprint | Training-free decoding-time defense，用 hidden-state trajectory 滑动窗口实时量化风险，12 种攻击上平均 95% 防御率，5.2ms/token 延迟 | **是 — Related Work** | Representation-based 方法，用 hidden states 做检测，与我们的 internal representation 路线直接相关；但它是 decoding-time single-turn defense，不涉及 multi-turn |\\n| SAE Jailbreak Mitigators (2602.12418) | Sparse Autoencoders are Capable LLM Jailbreak Mitigators | arXiv 2026-02 preprint | 提出 CC-Delta，用 SAE sparse features 做 inference-time mean-shift steering 防御越狱，在 sparse space 优于 dense steering，尤其 OOD 攻击 | **是 — Related Work** | Representation-based + interpretability 路线（SAE features），与我们的 representation mechanism 分析有方法论关联；他们做 steering 而非 detection，但 sparse feature 视角值得引用 |\\n| Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace (2602.11495) | Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace: Understanding and Detecting Jailbreak Attacks from Internal Representations of LLMs | arXiv 2026-02 preprint | 用 tensor decomposition 做 layer-wise 分析，发现越狱和正常 prompt 在内部表征有 layer-dependent 签名差异，跨 GPT-J/LLaMA/Mistral/Mamba 验证 | **是 — Related Work（高优先）** | 与我们最直接相关：都是从 internal representation 理解 jailbreak detection 机制。他们用 tensor decomposition 做可解释性，我们用 discriminative compression；互为补充视角 |\\n| Alon & Kamfonas 2023 (2308.14132) | Detecting Language Model Attacks with Perplexity | arXiv 2023-08 preprint | 用 GPT-2 计算 input perplexity 检测 adversarial suffix 越狱，对 suffix 攻击有效但对 human-crafted 攻击失败 | **可选引用** | 经典 perplexity-based baseline，方法论完全不同（surface-level statistical signal vs. internal representation）；在 Related Work 一句话提及 surface-level detection 方法即可 |\\n| Phute et al. 2023 (2308.07308) | LLM Self Defense: By Self Examination, LLMs Know They Are Being Tricked | ICLR 2024 Tiny Paper | 让 LLM 自检生成内容是否有害，无需 fine-tuning，attack success rate 降至~0% | **可选引用** | Output-based self-examination 方法，与我们的 input-side representation detection 路线不同；可在 Related Work 作为 output-side defense 的代表一句话提及 |\\n\\n---\\n\\n**总结判断：**\\n- **J-DAPT** 与我们的工作问题域差异大（robotic multimodal vs. LLM text multi-turn），但名字中的 \\\"DAPT\\\" 容易引起混淆，建议在论文中明确区分，Related Work 中简短提及其跨域适应思路。\\n- **高优先引用**：Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace（representation mechanism 最相关）、TrajGuard（hidden-state detection 路线）、SAE Mitigators（sparse representation 视角）\\n- **可选引用**：Alon & Kamfonas（perplexity baseline）、Phute（self-examination baseline）\\n\\nSources:\\n- [J-DAPT (arXiv:2509.23281)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23281)\\n- [J-DAPT Project Page](https://j-dapt.github.io/)\\n- [TrajGuard (arXiv:2604.07727)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07727)\\n- [SAE Jailbreak Mitigators (arXiv:2602.12418)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12418)\\n- [Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace (arXiv:2602.11495)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11495)\\n- [Alon & Kamfonas 2023 (arXiv:2308.14132)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.14132)\\n- [Phute et al. 2023 (arXiv:2308.07308)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.07308)\"}}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

分析有方法论关联；他们做 steering 而非 detection，但 sparse feature 视角值得引用 |\\n| Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace (2602.11495) | Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace: Understanding and Detecting Jailbreak Attacks from Internal Representations of LLMs | arXiv 2026-02 preprint | 用 tensor decomposition 做 layer-wise 分析，发现越狱和正常 prompt 在内部表征有 layer-dependent 签名差异，跨 GPT-J/LLaMA/Mistral/Mamba 验证 | **是 — Related Work（高优先）** | 与我们最直接相关：都是从 internal representation 理解 jailbreak detection 机制。他们用 tensor decomposition 做可解释性，我们用 discriminative compression；互为补充视角 |\\n| Alon & Kamfonas 2023 (2308.14132) | Detecting Language Model Attacks with Perplexity | arXiv 2023-08 preprint | 用 GPT-2 计算 input perplexity 检测 adversarial suffix 越狱，对 suffix 攻击有效但对 human-crafted 攻击失败 | **可选引用** | 经典 perplexity-based baseline，方法论完全不同（surface-level statistical signal vs. internal representation）；在 Related Work 一句话提及 surface-level detection 方法即可 |\\n| Phute et al. 2023 (2308.07308) | LLM Self Defense: By Self Examination, LLMs Know They Are Being Tricked | ICLR 2024 Tiny Paper | 让 LLM 自检生成内容是否有害，无需 fine-tuning，attack success rate 降至~0% | **可选引用** | Output-based self-examination 方法，与我们的 input-side representation detection 路线不同；可在 Related Work 作为 output-side defense 的代表一句话提及 |\\n\\n---\\n\\n**总结判断：**\\n- **J-DAPT** 与我们的工作问题域差异大（robotic multimodal vs. LLM text multi-turn），但名字中的 \\\"DAPT\\\" 容易引起混淆，建议在论文中明确区分，Related Work 中简短提及其跨域适应思路。\\n- **高优先引用**：Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace（representation mechanism 最相关）、TrajGuard（hidden-state detection 路线）、SAE Mitigators（sparse representation 视角）\\n- **可选引用**：Alon & Kamfonas（perplexity baseline）、Phute（self-examination baseline）\\n\\nSources:\\n- [J-DAPT (arXiv:2509.23281)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23281)\\n- [J-DAPT Project
```text
Both workers are done. Let me compile the results and report to Director. Key findings:

**J-DAPT:**
- It's about robotic multimodal jailbreak defense, not LLM text multi-turn detection
- Completely different problem domain (robotic vs LLM text)
- No overlap with our key contributions (label sensitivity, representation mechanism, benign adaptation, two-axis decomposition)
- Should be mentioned briefly in Related Work to differentiate

**Supplementary citations:**
- High priority: Jailbreaking Leaves a Trace (representation mechanism), TrajGuard (hidden-state), SAE Mitigators (sparse representation)


- Optional: Alon & Kamfonas (perplexity), Phute (self-examination)

**ToxicChat error analysis:**
The 9-class baseline struggles with topic confusion on benign inputs, misclassifying legitimate questions as jailbreak strategies, while missing single-turn attacks that lack the multi-turn persuasion patterns our model detects. Meanwhile, expanded_1000 is completely distinct from the original 349-sample dataset with zero overlap, which fundamentally changes the evaluation dynamics.

**Performance implications:**
The performance degradation appears driven by distribution shift rather than dataset size, and the reviewer's proposed control experiment isn't feasible to implement. I should report these findings to the Director.
```

       ```latex
       \paragraph{Confounds and transferability.}
       Three observations argue against a topic shortcut: unstable topic OOD (F1\,=\,0.541$\pm$0.207), scrambled labels within 3.9\,pp, and label-free MLM detection (F1\,$\geq$\,0.97). ToxicChat (+18--42\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:human_data}) and cross-LLM experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_llm}) confirm transfer; mixed-LLM 9-class maintains DD F1\,=\,0.818$\pm$0.096 while MLM collapses (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_llm}). Classification supervision reduces variance on synthetic OOD (4--20$\times$) but increases it on ToxicChat (std\,=\,0.087 vs.\ 0.013); frozen probing localizes MLM instability to Stage-2 (Appendix~\ref{app:frozen_verify}). TF-IDF collapses on ActorAttack (0.261 vs.\ 0.961 on DD).

       \paragraph{Mechanism: discriminative compression.}
       Classification DAPT compresses the feature space into few discriminative dimensions (participation ratio 3.7--9.4 vs.\ 25--32 for MLM/vanilla) while increasing class separability by 3--5 orders of magnitude (Fisher ratio 2.6--19.7 vs.\ $<$0.001; Appendix~\ref{app:representation}). Weight distances are comparable across variants ($\sim$0.2--0.4\%), so the effect is representational restructuring, not parameter-space regularization. Scrambled labels produce comparable compression (Fisher\,=\,2.6), confirming that classification structure drives the effect~\citep{chen2020simclr,grill2020byol,zhang2021understanding,maennel2020random}. Frozen probing near-parity (AA gap 3.1\,pp) and mean pooling matching BiGRU ($|\Delta| < 1$\,pp) corroborate that discriminative structure is established in Stage-1. This advantage is condition-specific: on single-turn human data (ToxicChat), MLM generalizes better (Section~\ref{sec:human_data}).

       \paragraph{Two-axis generalization.}
       OOD F1 reflects attack-side generalization under IID benign conditions. The orthogonal challenge is benign-side shift: DAPT increases WildChat FPR from 48.1\% (vanilla) to 68.1\% (Appendix~\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}), confirming adaptation amplifies benign sensitivity. Ten target-domain conversations reduce FPR to 0\% (95\% CI upper 6.1\%) without detection degradation (Appendix~\ref{app:benign_mixed}).

       \section{Conclusion}
       Controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations decomposes multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT via discriminative compression of the feature space; unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM data) and benign-side (10 target-domain conversations eliminate WildChat FPR 68.1\%\,$\rightarrow$\,0\%). Label sensitivity is attack-complexity-dependent (TOST equivalence on DD/FITD; $d$\,=\,0.44 on ActorAttack); temporal order and label granularity are non-factors. Combined with 24.3$\times$ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\ref{app:efficiency}), this yields a deployment recipe requiring no target-domain attack data.
       ```

ToxicChat validates transfer to human-authored single-turn attacks (+18--42\\,pp over vanilla), but no human-generated multi-turn jailbreak dataset exists. On WildChat, 9-class FPR reaches 68.1\\% (Appendix~\\ref{app:wildchat_fpr}). This limitation is addressable: a benign adaptation curve (Appendix~\\ref{app:benign_mixed}) shows that as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate FPR without detection degradation, though validation on larger and more diverse OOD benign samples is needed. The 10-conversation benign adaptation result is validated only on WildChat (GPT-3.5 user conversations); whether it generalizes to other LLM assistants or enterprise chat systems is unevaluated. Residual calibration error (ECE\\,=\\,0.126--0.141; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}) suggests temperature scaling is needed for deployment.\n\nThree encoders (125M--355M) differ in architecture and pretraining; same-architecture scaling is needed to isolate the capacity effect, and whether findings hold at LLM-scale encoders (7B+) remains open. We acknowledge dual-use risk, as the persuasion taxonomy could inform adversarial actors. We mitigate this by using only synthetic conversations (no real harmful content), withholding attack-generation prompts, and focusing contributions on detection mechanisms.         "content": "% abstract.tex — D048 deep writing optimization\n\\begin{abstract}\nDetecting unseen multi-turn jailbreaks requires domain adaptation, but the form of adaptation matters.\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks spread adversarial intent across benign-seeming turns, evading existing defenses: Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks.\nWe isolate the factors behind cross-attack generalization on synthetic multi-turn data through controlled ablation: thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125 to 355M parameters) decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, and granularity on three out-of-distribution (OOD) attack families and two training compositions.\nOn single-source synthetic data, continued pretraining closes roughly 60 percentage points of the OOD gap without labels. This effectiveness is condition-dependent: on mixed-LLM training data, unsupervised pretraining collapses while classification-based adaptation remains the strongest variant (DD OOD F1\\,=\\,0.818 vs.\\ 0.291 without adaptation).\nAcross three architectures (125--355M), attacks requiring adaptation shrink with scale, though architecture differences prevent isolating scale alone.\nClassification supervision reduces prediction variance on synthetic data with attack-complexity-dependent label sensitivity, but this benefit reverses on human-authored data.\nOrthogonal to attack-side generalization, benign distribution shift is a practical bottleneck: WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\\%, but as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate false positives (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%).\nThese findings decompose multi-turn jailbreak generalization into two orthogonal axes: attack-side (classification DAPT compresses representations into few discriminative dimensions) and benign-side (solved by few-shot adaptation), with 24.3$\\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
```json
        "numLines": 12,

      }
```

```json
        "content": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Pattern Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants systematically vary training objectives while sharing the same two-stage pipeline (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions reveals two orthogonal generalization axes. On the \\emph{attack side}, vanilla DeBERTa drops to F1\\,=\\,0.28--0.31 on OOD attacks; classification DAPT recovers to 0.97--1.0, while MLM collapses under mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla). On the \\emph{benign side}, distributional mismatch drives false positives: WildChat FPR reaches 68.1\\%, but as few as 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate it (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%). RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Two-axis generalization decomposition.} We identify two orthogonal generalization challenges in multi-turn jailbreak detection. Attack-side: controlled ablation across thirteen encoder configurations reveals classification DAPT as the most robust variant (vanilla F1\\,=\\,0.28--0.31 on OOD $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0); unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. Benign-side: 10 target-domain benign conversations eliminate WildChat FPR from 68.1\\% to 0\\% (95\\% CI upper 6.1\\%) without detection degradation.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Condition-dependent adaptation effectiveness and constraining negative results.} MLM is effective on single-source data but collapses under mixed-LLM training; classification DAPT is robust across conditions. Three supplementary negative results constrain which properties matter: scrambled labels perform comparably to correct labels on template-based attacks (TOST-confirmed on DD and FITD at $n$\\,=\\,10, $d$\\,=\\,0.44 on structurally complex ActorAttack), temporal order is irrelevant ($\\Delta < 1$\\,pp), and label granularity does not affect detection.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Practical deployment recipe.} Continued pretraining is 24.3$\\times$ faster than LLM-as-judge (Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}), and 10-shot benign adaptation eliminates domain-specific false positives. No target-domain attack data is required for deployment.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 30
      }
```
